products. The document can be downloaded from the www.io -link.com  portal.

shall be possible. It is the responsibility of the IO-Link Device manufacturer to test the IODD file with the 
help of the IODD -Checker tool available per dow nload from www.io -link.com .  
NOTE 3 Any IO -Link devices shall provide an associated manufacturer declaration on the conformity of the device 
with this specification, its related IODD,  and test documents, available per download from www.io -link.com .

 1 
0 Introduction  2 
 0.1 General  3 
The base technology of IO -Link ™1) is subject matter of the international standard IEC 61131 -9 (see  [2]). It 4 
specifies a single -drop digital communication interface technology for small sensors and actuators – named 5 
SDCI, which extends the traditional switching input and output interfaces as defined in IEC 61131 -2 towards 6 
a point -to-point communication link  using coded switching. This technology enables the cyclic exchange of 7 
digital input and output process data between a Master and its associated W -Devices (sensors, actuators, 8 
I/O terminals, etc.). The Master can be part of a fieldbus communication system or any stand -alone 9 
 
1  IO-Link TM and IO-Link Wireless TM are trade names of the "IO -Link Community". This information is given for the convenience of 
users of this specification and does not constitute an endorsement by the IO -Link Community of the trade name holder or any of 
its products. Compliance to this st andard does not require use of the registered logos for IO -Link TM. Use of the registered logos 
for IO -Link TM requires permission of the "IO -Link Community".  

===PAGE 12===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 12 of 330  
  
processing unit. The technology also enables  the acyclic transfer of parameters to Devices and the 10 
propagation of diagnosis information from the Devices to the upper -level automation system (controller, 11 
host) via the Master/gateway.  12 
This  document provides the necessary changes and extensions to the basic IO -Link interface and system 13 
standard for wireless communication including the radio characteristic, air interface, frequencies, 14 
message/frame types, and pairing mechanism as well as the necessary configuration management and the 15 
changes of state machines compared to IO-Link Interface and System Specification .  16 
 17 
IEC 61131 -9 is part of a series of standards on programmable controllers and the associated peripherals 18 
and should be read in con junction with the other parts of the series.  19 
Terms of general use are defined in IEC  61131 -1 or in the IEC  60050 series. More specific terms are defined 20 
in each part.  21 
Conformity with this document cannot be claimed unless the requirements of Annex I  are me t. 22 
The main characteristics of the IO -Link Wireless technology are:  23 
• The application interface for cyclic (Process Data) and acyclic data (On -request Data) is compatible 24 
to IO -Link; from the user perspective, it is a transparent view on W -Devices.  25 
• A W -Maste r can handle up to 5 transmission W-Tracks  in parallel, each W-Track can handle a 26 
maximum of 8 W -Devices, thus supporting up to 40 W -Devices per W -Master . 27 
• Up to 3 W -Master can be placed in a cell, yielding a maximum of 120 W -Devices per W -Master cell.  28 
• A scan service is available for discovery of yet unpaired W -Devices.  29 
• A pairing service is provided to assign W -Devices to a W -Master, corresponding to a logical cable 30 
connection.  31 
• There are no limitations for typical relative movement speeds of W -Devices with in a single W -Master 32 
cell.  33 
• Controlled roaming between multiple W -Master cells is supported by a dedicated handover 34 
mechanism.  35 
• A minimum transmission cycle time of 5 ms can support high -speed wireless applications with a 36 
payload of up to 32 octets.  37 
• IO-Link Wireless also supports mechanisms for low energy W -Devices.  38 
• IO-Link Wireless  utilizes radios for the 2,4 GHz ISM band, divided to frequency channels with a 39 
spacing of 1 MHz.  40 
• Frequency Hopping changes the frequency channels for each transmission as a measu re against 41 
interference, yielding a RFP of 10 -9 which is similar to a wired connection.  42 
• W-Coexistence with other wireless systems ( e.g.,  WLAN) is achieved with a blocklisting  mechanism.  43 
• To comply with regulatory standards, transmission power is limited to  ≤ 10 dBm (10 mW) EIRP, still 44 
yielding a range of up to 20 m in case of a W -Master cell with one W-Track. In case of more than 45 
one W-Track, 10  m can be achieved. These figures are dependent on the machine environment.  46 
• Each transmission W-Track in a W -Maste r can use its own narrow -band transceiver and dedicated 47 
antenna or all of them can use a single shared transceiver and/or antenna.  48

1 Scope  72 
This document specifies IO -Link Wireless communication for factory automation. Different aspects of 73 
communication are realized by different communication layers based on the following layer model.  74 
 75 
The “PL” (Air interface) for IO-Link Wireless  includes the Physical Layer as well as the wireless mechanisms 76 
(e.g.,  pairing, blocklisting , …) with all aspects related to the radio:  77 
• Antenna aspects  78 
• Radio transceivers  79 
• Radio freque ncies  80 
• Bidirectional data transmission via downlink and uplink (W -Sub-cycle)  81 
Media access and frequency hopping patterns  82 
W-Sub-cycle structures  83 
Following elements specify the Data Link Layer (DL):  84 
• Data scheduling (DL -A) 85 
• Data handling (DL -B) 86 
Following eleme nts specify the Application Layer (AL):  87 
• Data exchange  88 
System Management (SM) realizes:  89 
• Operating states  90 
• Pairing functionality for W -Master and its W -Devices during commissioning and replacement  91 
• Parameterization (download of W -Parameters)  92 
 93 
In addition, this document provides the necessary changes and extensions to the IO-Link Interface and 94 
System Specification  for the operation of wireless communication.  95 
 96

3.3 Conventions  421 
 General  422 
The service model, service primitives, and the diagrams shown in this document  are entirely abstract 423 
descriptions. Th e implementation of the services may reflect individual issues and can be different.  424 
 Service primitives  425 
Service primitives are used to represent service provider/consumer interactions (ISO/IEC 10731). Each 426 
service consists of up to four service primitives:  427 
• request primitive (.req),  428 
• indication primitive (.ind),  429 
• response primitive (.rsp), and  430 
• confirmation primitive (.cnf).  431 
An indication can occur with or without the corresponding response, a request can occur with or without the 432 
corresponding confirma tion.  433 
 434 
Figure 2 shows a generalized example of a confirmed service. In this case “Initiator” and “Responder” 435 
correspond to either Master or Device and not to the layer within one of each.  436 
Figure 2 Generalized example of a confirmed service  437 
 438 
 Service parameters  439 
Service primitives  convey parameters which indicate the information available in the provider/ consumer 440 
interaction. In any particular interface, not each and every parameter needs to be explicitly stated.  441 


===PAGE 23===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 23 of 330  
  
The service specification in this document  uses a tabular format to  describe the component parameters of 442 
the service primitives. The parameters which apply to each group of service primitives are set out in tables. 443 
Each table consists of up to five columns:  444 
1) parameter name;  445 
2) request primitive (.req);  446 
3) indication p rimitive (.ind);  447 
4) response primitive (.rsp); and  448 
5) confirmation primitive (.cnf).  449 
One parameter (or component of it) is listed in each row of each table. Under the appropriate service 450 
primitive columns, a code is used to specify the type of usage of th e parameter on the primitive specified 451 
in the column.  452 
M  Parameter is mandatory for the primitive.  453 
U  Parameter is a user option and can or cannot be provided depending on dynamic usage of the 454 
service user. When not provided a default value for the paramet er is assumed.  455 
C  Parameter is conditional upon other parameters or upon the environment of the service user.  456 
–  Parameter is never present.  457 
S  Parameter is a selected item.  458 
Some entries are further qualified by items in brackets. These can be:  459 
a)  a param eter -specific constraint "(=)" indicates that the parameter is semantically equivalent to the 460 
parameter in the service primitive to its immediate left in the table;  461 
b)  an indication that some note applies to the entry "(n)" indicates that the following no te "n" contains 462 
additional information related to the parameter and its use.  463 
 Service procedures  464 
The procedures are defined in terms of:  465 
• the interactions between application entities through the exchange of protocol data units; and  466 
•  the interactions between a communication layer service provider and a communication layer service 467 
consumer in the same system through the invocation of service primitives.  468 
These procedures are applicable to instances of communication between systems which supp ort time -469 
constrained communications services within the communication layers.  470 
 Service attributes  471 
The nature of the different ( W-Master and W-Device) services is characterized by attributes. All services 472 
are defined from the view of the affected layer towar ds the layer above.  473 
I  Initiator of a service  (.ind)  (towards the layer above)  474 
R  Receiver (responder) of a service  (.req)  (from the layer above)  475 
 476 
Figure 3 shows the example of initiator / receiver of services (W -Device)  477

Figure 3 Example of initiator / receiver of services (W -Device)  479

• a request and the corresponding confirmation  (the request being in the direction of the arrow);  484 
• an in dicatio n and the corresponding response ( the indication  bein g in the direction of the 485 
arrow)  486

DL_SetMode_INACTIVE. For sequence diagrams, the service primitive is attached via an underscore 507

character instead of a dot, and the service parameter is added in parenthesis, such as for example in 508

4 Overview of IO-Link Wireless  513 
4.1 Purpose and topology  514 
IO-Link Wireless  is a communication technology intended to replace the cable(s) for remote sensor/actuator 515 
control in production automation. The key features of IO-Link Wireless  technology are real -time capabilities, 516 
very low latency and robustness. Applications within fa ctory automation comprise moving parts such as 517 
rotating bottle filling, robot arms and linear moving machinery. These applications are difficult to realize 518 
with wired sensor/actuator equipment or suffer from frequently broken wires. These kinds of applicat ions 519 
are targets of IO-Link Wireless . 520 
IO-Link Wireless  equipment operates in the unlicensed 2 ,4 GHz industrial, scientific and medical application 521 
(ISM) band and using frequency hopping to reduce the impact of interference. IO-Link Wireless  specifies 522 
the communication between the IO-Link Wireless Maste r (W-Master) and one or more IO-Link Wireless 523 
Devices  (W-Devices).  524 
 525 
 526 
Figure 6 IO-Link and IO-Link Wireless  topology  527 
From a PLC or Controller users’  point of view, Master and W -Master  provide the same functionality in 528 
respect to Process Data (PD) and On -request Data (OD). The main differences between the two topologies 529 
is during commissioning, the discovery of available W -Devices ("scan"), the connection configuration 530 
("pairing") and t he parameterization of the air interface ("W -Parameter").  531 
  532 


===PAGE 27===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 27 of 330  
  
4.2 Positioning in the automation hierarchy  533 
 General  534 
Figure 7 shows the architecture of an auto mation topology with an IO-Link Wireless  system comparable to 535 
an IO -Link system . 536 
 537 
 538 
Figure 7 IO-Link Wireless  system  539 
In all cases a PLC, a PC -based controller, or an embedded system can exchange Process Data (PD) and/or 540 
On-request Data (OD) with wire or radio connected devices via Master or W -Master, respectively.  541 
Additional to the IO-Link Interface and System Specification  ([1]) cla use 13.5  the “Port and Device 542 
Configuration Tool” (PDCT) for IO -Link can be extended by features like:  543 
• Device discovery and pairing support for unpaired devices.  544 
• Optimizing connection quality of W -Masters and W -Devices.  545 
• W-Coexistence management for a conf lict -free layout of the radio transmissions such as overlapping 546 
frequencies of non -IO-Link Wireless  systems.  547 
• Configuration of the W -Parameters as described in the provided IODDs.  548 
IO-Link Wireless  uses the Data Storage mechanism of IO-Link Interface and S ystem Specification  [1] to 549 
support faulty  W-Device  replacement. To re -establish a wireless connection after a W -Device replacement, 550 
pairing buttons or software tools can be used. With pairing buttons activated on both W -Device and W -551 
Master a W -Device can be exchanged without the need of any software tools .  552 
After replacement, the parameters are downloaded automatically from the Data Storage, if enabled.  553

 Role of a W -Master  561 
A W -Master manages up to 40 W -Port instances. The possible max. number of W -Ports depends on the 562 
available W-Tracks  and slots and how they are utilized.  563 
A W -Master can c omprise up to five (small band) transceivers with their own antenna and dedicated 564 
frequency channels, called W-Tracks . Each W-Track can serve up to 8 W -Devices and send and receive 565 
alternately. All W-Tracks  of a W -Master send at the same time on different frequencies according to the 566 
computed frequency hopping tables, providing an optimal medium utilization.  567 
The user may manually operate the W -Master for discovery and pairing of  W-Device s. 568 
During commissioning or roaming Service Mode is used by the W -Master  to establish communication with 569 
W-Devices (pairing), including checking of the "identity" of the W -Device, i.e.,  its VendorID, DeviceID, and 570 
communication properties. If there is a mismatch between W -Device parameters and the stored parameter 571 
set within t he W-Master, the parameters in the W -Device are overwritten (see 11.4 ) or the stored parameters 572 
within the W -Master are updated depending on the configuration.  573 
After power on with paired W -Devices, the W -Master establishes communication, including all checks 574 
described above.  575 
The W -Master is responsible for the assembly and disassembly of all data from or to the W -Devices (see 576 
Clause 11). 577 
 Role of a W -Device  578 
A W-Device consists of a single transceiver, the IOLW device stack and the technology specific application, 579 
i.e.,  the transducer with its technology parameters. The commo n W-Device applications are the same as in 580 
IO-Link and comprise of configuration parameters, diagnosis information and process data.  581 
 582 
 Role of a W -Bridge  583 
A W-Bridge is a W -Device to connect a single IO -Link Device. The application part of the W -Bridge basi cally 584 
contains an IO-Link Master.  585 
For compatibility reasons towards the IO-Link Device in the System Configuration Tooling, a straightforward  586 
parameter mapping of the IO-Link Device via the W -Bridge is desired.  587 
 588 
 System Configuration Tool  589 
Engineering support for a W -Master is usually provided by a Port and Device Configuration Tool (PDCT). 590 
The PDCT configures both W -Port and W -Device properties. It combines both an interpreter of the IO Device 591 
Description (IODD) and a configurator (see 11.8.2 ). The parameters provide all the necessary properties to 592 
establish communication and the desired function of a sensor or actuator.  593 
 Mapping to fie ldbuses  594 
See clause 4.7 in [1] and clause 11.9.1 . 595 
4.3 Cell concept  596 
Due to the limited transmission power (see Air Interface 4.5), the possible range of a W -Master is limited 597 
to max. 20 m in case of only one W-Track as shown in Figure 9. This value is derating to ≤ 10 m if more 598 
than one W-Track  is active.  599 
 600 
Figure 9 Radius of a cell with a 1 W-Track W -Master  601 
 602 
A single W -Master can consist of one up to five W-Tracks . Up to 3 W -Masters are allowed within one cell 603 
to a certain extent. If there are more than one W -Master installed in a cell, the MasterID’s shall be 604 
conse cutive . To prevent frequency access conflicts between the W-Tracks , IO-Link Wireless  provides 605 
mechanisms to create disjoint frequency tables by W -Masters. Every W -Master has its MasterID, a 606 
frequency hopping table and a blocklist .  607 
W-Device
W-Master
1 W-Track
W-Device
max. 20 m max. 20 m

===PAGE 30===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 30 of 330  
  
One W -Master and a group  of associated W -Devices form a W -Master cell as shown in Figure 10. The W -608 
Master A is connected to W -Devices A1 to Ai. The W -Master B is connected to W-Devices B1 to B j, whereas 609 
both systems are in an overlapping RF coverage area.  610 
 611 
Figure 10 Cell consisting of 2 W -Master  cells  612 
 613 
Figure 11 shows the IO-Link Wireless  concept with partly overlapping W -Master RF coverage areas  . In 614 
one area, there should not exist more than three W -Master s in order to avoid interference. W -Master cells 615 
with a distance of more than 40  m can use the same MasterID again. W -Master s with a distance less than 616 
40 m require distinct MasterIDs. The MasterID is used to calculate individual frequency hopping tables.  617 
 618 
Figure 11 Cell concept  619 
4.4 Wireless Mechanisms  620 
 General  621 
The following mechanisms are used to setup and operate the wireless connections.  622 
 623 
 Scan (W-Device  Discovery)  624 
After power -on, every unpaired W -Device is waiting for connection est ablishment from a W -Master on the 625 
configuration frequency channels. Upon user request for W -Device discovery, the W -Master sends scan 626 
request messages on the configuration frequency channels. Any unpaired W -Device receiving such a scan 627 
request message is r esponding with a scan response message, where the W -Device returns its unique 628 
identifier (UniqueID) for authentication purposes before pairing. With the help of this mechanism all 629 
unpaired W -Devices in the proximity of the W -Master can be discovered. Subse quently, the application can 630 
decide to pair the W -Devices.  631

Several W -Devices may simultaneously respond within a n uplink  slot . In order to minimize collisions, they 632 
are using randomly determined time slot positions within that uplink frequency. In this man ner, the W -Master 633 
collects all non -paired W -Devices over time within several W -Sub-cycles.  634 
 Pairing  635 
Pairing is the equivalent procedure to plug in the cable connection in a wired system .  636 
 637 
4.4.3.1  Pairing by UniqueID  638 
This mechanism is provided for pairing of a W -Device with a pre -configured W -Master and reflects the 639 
normal commissioning mechanism. The UniqueID of the W -Device is used for automatic identification of 640 
the W -Device within the pairing process. An Engineerin g Tool or HMI such as an PDCT is required for the 641 
pre-configuration of the W -Master. See clause 10.7.3 .  642 
4.4.3.2  Pairing by Button  643 
This mechanism is for ma nual pairing without detailed knowledge about the W -Device. No Engineering Tool 644 
is required for this kind of pairing. The pairing must be acknowledged on both entities by manual intervention 645 
(i.e.,  pressing a button or equivalent mechanism). In case of a faulty W -Device, which must be replaced 646 
with a new, but identical W -Device, IO-Link Wireless  provides this simplified procedure for the pa iring of 647 
both partners without using an Engineering Tool or PDCT.  648 
4.4.3.3  Re-Pairing by Button  649 
A W-Device previously paired to former W -Master can be re -paired to a new W -Master. If such a W -Device 650 
still has the ConnectionParameters of its “old” W -Master, it stays in state Configured. With a button press, 651 
the W -Device can be switched by the operator temporarily to state Re_Pairing and listens on the 652 
configuration channels for a pairing request message from the new W -Master. The pairing shall be started 653 
previously  on the W -Master by manual intervention ( i.e.,  by UniqueID or pressing a button).  654 
 Unpairing  655 
A W-Device can be removed from a communication relationship with a W -Master. When the operator wants 656 
to unpair one of the W -Devices, the W -Master is triggered by the operator ( i.e.,  via HMI) and starts an 657 
unpairing procedure o n the dedicated W -Device. This sends an unpairing request to the W -Device, which 658 
sends an acknowledgement back to the W -Master. The ConnectionParameters on the W -Device and the 659 
related W -Port configuration within the W -Master are deleted.  660 
 Roaming  661 
Roaming is a feature that allows mobility to a predefined W -Device between multiple predefined W -Master 662 
cells. A W -Master W-Track  configured to Roaming Mode is sending scan request messages on the 663 
configuration channels to detect roaming W -Devices in their range. Disconnected roaming W -Devices 664 
listening for a W -Master shall respond with a scan response message to indicate their presence to this W -665 
Master. The application on the W -Masters can then decide to connect the roaming W -Device by initiating a 666 
pairing and con figuration sequence.  667 
The support of this function is mandatory for a W -Device and optional for a W -Master.  668 
Handover disconnect is initiated by the W -Master when the application ( e.g.,  the PLC) wants to release the 669 
W-Device, for example when the applicatio n has finished processing with the roaming W -Device in its 670 
current state ( e.g.,  in a tool changer or conveyor belt application). Another reason for a disconnect 671 
procedure could be that the Wireless Quality of the W -Port has degraded to an absolute minimum,  indicating 672 
that the W -Device leaves the range of the W -Master. Reconnection in the case of link quality degradation 673 
to the same W -Master should only be done if the link quality has improved substantially.  674 
For any handover procedure with another W -Master,  the scan message followed by a pairing and a W -675 
Device startup sequence is utilized.  676 
NOTE: The handover procedure requests a certain amount of time where no process data can be 677 
exchanged.  678 
An unexpected IMA -Failure detected by a W -Master from a roaming W -Device shall lead to an autonomous 679 
handover disconnect of this W -Device for the associated W -Port within the W -Master.  680 

===PAGE 32===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 32 of 330  
  
 681 
 682 
Figure 12 Roaming between W -Master cells  683 
 684 
“Roaming” is configurable on the W -Master. On each W -Master, not more than one W-Track  shall be 685 
configured for Roaming Mode, as indicated in  Figure 12 for W -Master 1 and W -Master 3. The W-Tracks  in 686 
Roaming Mode utilize a dedicated frequency hopping table which includes the configuration channels. For 687 
the “handover disconnect” procedure, the entire fault indications ( e.g.,  IMA timeout) to the system/user are 688 
suppressed,  since it is related to an intended action. Accordingly, all pending diagnosis messages of the 689 
related W -Port and W -Device are deleted once the "handover disconnect" procedure is completed. A 690 
roaming W -Device does not permanently store its pairing informat ion and discards it when disconnected.  691 
The computation of the frequency hopping tables for roaming is described in clause  H.2 692 
 693 
 Automatic Pairing  694 
Automatic pairing (AutoPairing) is a feature that allows the pairing of W -Devices to W -Ports directly after 695 
the configuration is set without the need for additional user  interaction. The automatic pairing is configured 696 
in the "PortMode" element in parameter "WPortConfigList" (see E.1.2). If the “PortMode” element is set to 697 
“Cyclic AutoPairing” or “Roaming AutoPairing”, the AutoPairing is activated for this port.  698 
The behav ior of the AutoPairing mechanism depends on the active mode of the service track of the W -699 
Master:  700 
• If the service track is in cyclic mode, the pairing is triggered once after the W -Port configuration is 701 
set. If the configured W -Device is not available at th at time, the pairing procedure will fail. The user 702 
will have to start the pairing manually again when the W -Device is available.  703 
• If the service track is in roaming mode, the pairing is triggered once after the W -Port configuration 704 
is set. If the W -Device i s not available at that time, the pairing procedure will fail. As the W -Master 705 


===PAGE 33===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 33 of 330  
  
is permanently scanning for W -Devices in this mode, the pairing can be automatically triggered 706 
again, when the configured W -Device is found in the scan results.  707 
The used pairing  method for AutoPairing is always “Pairing by UniqueID” as described in c lause  4.4.3.1  708 
 Transmission Error Handling  709 
Transmitted packets in both uplink and downlink direction are subject to error detection via CRC and shall 710 
be acknowledged by the receiving side. W -Devices acknowledge correct reception of their Downlink packets 711 
within the respective subsequent Uplink pack ets. Within the next downlink, the W -Master acknowledges 712 
correct reception of the Uplink packet to each W -Device. In case of missing acknowledgments, the W -713 
Master uses this information to initiate a retransmission within the same W -Cycle. When all retransm issions 714 
fail within a W -Cycle, a communication error is indicated towards system management.  715 
 716 
 “I am alive” supervision (IMA)  717 
The activity of a W -Device is monitored via an “I am alive” (IMA) timer in the W -Master. When a W -Device 718 
has no data to transmit f or a time period longer than its configured IMATime, an IMA message shall be sent 719 
by the W -Device before the IMA -timer expires. When the supervision IMA -timer of the W -Master expires, 720 
i.e.,  because the W -Device is down, a communication error shall be indic ated via system management.  721 
 Wireless Quality supervision  722 
Wireless Quality is used for evaluation of the functionality and reliability of the IO -Link Wireless system in 723 
its application environment of the wireless connection. The parameter WirelessQuality of a communication 724 
link between W -Master and a W -Device is continuously monitored an d can be accessed on W -Master by 725 
the gateway application via service GetPortQuality.  726 
4.5 Concept of Air Interface  727 
 General  728 
IO-Link Wireless  uses the license -free 2 ,4 GHz ISM band (industrial, scientific, and medical) from 2 ,4 to 729 
2,4835 GHz compliant to [5] Bluetooth SIG - Regulatory Committee, "Bluetooth low energy Regulatory 730 
Aspects", V10r00, 26 April 2011, which also forms the basis for the well -known Bluetooth ®-technol ogy. It 731 
is therefore possible to cost -effectively use existing integrated radio circuits available on the market to build 732 
IO-Link Wireless  systems. But it must be noted that there is no system compatibility between Bluetooth© 733 
and IO-Link Wireless . For more  details see clause 5.4  734 
 Frequency Division Multiple Access (FDMA)  735 
Using different carrier frequencies in IO-Link Wireless  follows in principle the Frequency Division Multiple 736 
Access (FDMA) technology. IO-Link Wireless  periodically changes the transmission  frequencies 737 
("frequency hopping") to improve robustness against burst interferences. W-Coexistence with other wireless 738 
systems and other IO-Link Wireless  cells is possible through omitting those frequencies within the table of 739 
unusable transmission freque ncies (" Blocklisting "). 740 
4.5.2.1  Frequency Hopping Tables  741 
To compute the frequency hopping tables for a W -Master and its W -Devices, IO-Link Wireless  defines 742 
dedicated channel hopping sequence algorithms that depend on the individual MasterID to achieve W-743 
Coexistenc e within neighboring IO-Link Wireless  systems. For W-Coexistence with other wireless systems, 744 
a blocklist  can be utilized to avoid certain frequency channels in the computed hopping table. The hopping 745 
sequence is transferred to the W -Device during pairing. For more details, see clause H.2 746 
4.5.2.2  Blocklist  747 
To suspend  frequency ranges, they can be  defined in the blocklist . The blocklist  is configurable via 748 
SetMasterCon fig. For more details, see clause  H.1. 749 
4.5.2.3  Configuration channels  750 
Configuration of W -Master and W -Devices is required prior to cyclic data exchange. For this purpose, the 751 
frequency channels "1" (2401 MHz) and "80" (2480 MHz) are exclusively used in an alternating manner for 752 
ConnectionParameter exchange and initial scan and pairing of W -Master with its W -Devices. For a detailed 753 
description of usage of the configuration c hannels see H.3  754 
4.5.2.4  Data channels  755 
The frequency channels 3 (2403 MHz) to 78 (2478 MHz) can be used for cyclic data exchange. This number 756 
of frequency channels allows the configuration of W -Master sets disjoint from their cell neighbors for W-757 

===PAGE 34===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 34 of 330  
  
Coexistence. The set of frequency channels a W -Master uses is configured in the frequency hopping table 758 
(see H.2). 759 
 Time Division Multiple Access (TDMA)  760 
IO-Link Wireless  uses Time Division Multiple Access (TDMA) p rinciples. A communication exchange 761 
between a W -Master and its W -Devices is splitted into a "downlink" phase that is immediately followed by 762 
an "uplink" phase for a dedicated W-Track  and frequency channel. The transmitters on the W -Master and 763 
W-Devices are  operating in half -duplex mode, switching between Tx and Rx mode according to their time 764 
slots.  765 
TDMA requires precise timings on both, sender and receiver. At the beginning of a TDMA cycle, the 766 
frequency channel is selected from the hopping table. The W -Devices respond at their subsequent 767 
respective time slots using the same frequency channel.  768 
4.5.3.1  Downlink  769 
The downlink communication from W -Master to its W -Devices can contain W -Messages for several W -770 
Devices as shown in Figure 13. It is therefore a multicast communication. Immediately after sending the 771 
Downlink, the W -Master switches its radios from Tx to Rx mode, awaiting the subsequent uplink 772 
transmissions  from the W -Devices of that W-Track . 773 

===PAGE 35===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 35 of 330

4.5.3.3  Synchronization  782 
The W -Master provides th e system’s master clock  which is a downlink sent each W -Sub-cycle . To precisely 783 
switch the radio mode and send the Uplinks in the respective time  slots, the clocks of the W -Devices need 784 
to be continuously synchronized with the master clock. Synchronization  of a W -Device clock takes always 785 
place when the W -Device receives a downlink from the W -Master. When the clocks after a longer 786 
communication pause between W -Master and W -Device have deviated (this particularly happens using low 787 
energy W -Devices), the W -Device may listen for a longer period of time until it detects its W -Masters 788 
downlink again.  789 
A paired W -Device that has lost clock synchronization still knows the frequency channels of its W -Master 790 
via the frequency table. It just listens at one particular f requency channel until it receives an appropriate 791 
downlink of its W -Master and is then able to synchronize to the hopping sequence and uplink time slots.  792 
 SSlots, DSlots, Transmission capacity  793 
The transmission capacity of downlink and uplink is shown in Figure 15. The Downlink can carry 52 octets. 794 
An uplink can carry 12 octets or 25 octets, depending on the slot type “SSlot” or “DSlot”. DSlots combine 795 
two SSlots to operate sensors or actuators with larger process data, but this reduces the number of possible 796 
W-Devices per W-Track .  797 
 798 
Figure 15 Transmission capacity with SSlots and DSlots  799 
Some octets are required for protocol cont rol data and integrity checksums, finally reducing the usable 800 
message payload. The message payload encodings are listed in Annex D . 801 
 802

A W-Master contains up to 5 W-Tracks , which are numbered from 0 to 4. Each W-Track  has up to 8 slots, 804 
numbered from 0 to 7. This allows a maximum number of 40 W -Devices per W -Master.  805 
Figure 16 shows the assignment of W -Device numbers to slots and W-Tracks .  806 
The allocation of W -Devices to W-Track  and Slot number is performed during commissioning and pairing.  807

 Assignment of W -Ports to W -Devices  813 
A W-Master provides a limited number of virtual W -Ports, depending on the number of available W-Tracks  814 
and the slot configuration, since W -Devices with occupation of a DSlot reduce the number of available W -815 
Ports. The W -Master shall therefore administrativ ely map its W -Device slots to these virtual W -Ports, which 816 
is performed on application level during commissioning. The application shall maintain a monotonically 817 
increasing numbered list of W -Ports counting from 0 in the sequence of the commissioning opera tion and 818 
assign the W -Device slots autonomously. The mapping between W -Port and W -Device slot numbering is 819 
given via the W -Port handler of the System Management SM. The W-Track and W -Device -Mapper  (TD-820 
Mapper) uses this information to map a W -Port to the co rresponding W-Track  and slot (see 6.2.2 ). 821 
 822 
 W-Cycle  823 
A W -Cycle utilizes TDMA and FDMA in combination with a retransmission mechanism to achieve a very 824 
dependable wi reless transmission. The standard duration of a complete W -Cycle is almost 5 ms as shown 825 
in Figure 17, consisting of three W -Sub-cycles. The W -Cycle is configurable via SM_SetPortConfig with a 826 
granularity of the time duration of a W -Sub-cycle . 827 
The W-Master uses the remaining W -Sub-cycles for retries in case of transmission errors caused by channel 828 
interferences. The different frequency channels for these sub -cycles and for each W-Track  are the 829 
countermeasure against these channel interferences. When no retransmissions are required within a W -830 
Cycle, the otherwise unused bandwidth can be used to transfer acyclic data, such as On -request data (OD) 831 
or Events.  832 
A W -Master can comprise up to five (small band) transceivers with their own antenna and dedicated 833 
frequency channels, called a W-Track . Each  W-Track  can serve up to 8 W -Devices and send and receive 834 
alternately. All W-Tracks  of a W -Master send at the same time on different frequencies according to the 835 
computed frequency hopping tables, providing an optimal medium utilization . 836

A W -Frame is the data structure in which a communication exchange between a W -Master and its W -841 
Devices is organized (see Figure 18). It is structured in Control intervals, Downlink and Uplinks. In Control 842 
interval, the radio switches between transmission and reception and in the first Control interval also 843 
frequency hopping takes place.  844 
The Do wnlink addresses the W-Device s via multi cast. The Uplinks is transmitted subsequently W -Device 845 
by W -Device in the respective time  slot. The W -Frame is transmitted in a W -Sub-cycle of 1 ,664 ms. 846

5 Physical Layer (PL)  881 
5.1 General  882 
This clause describes the relevant definitions for transceivers and media access on both, W -Master and W -883 
Devices, which shall comply to the requirements described below.  884 
 885 
5.2 Base technology, Physical Layer (PL)  886 
 General  887 
IO-Link Wireless  uses frequencies from 2401 to 2480 MHz of the license -free 2 ,4 GHz ISM band (industrial, 888 
scientific, and medical).  889 
The Physical Layer of IO-Link Wireless  is based on the proven technology used in Bluetooth© version 4.2 890 
(Bluetooth Low Energy). It is therefore possible to use ra dios available on the market with the restriction 891 
that the requ irements, described in the following clauses, shall be taken in to account.  892 
 Transmission rate  893 
The on -air bit duration Tbit is 1 µs as shown in Figure 20. Hence, the gross transmission rate is 1 Mbit/s.  894 
 Carrier frequency accuracy  895 
The carrier frequencies fc of a W -Master or a W -Device shall not deviate more than +/ - 20 ppm.  896 
 W-Device Carrier frequency calibration  897 
W-Device adjusts their carrier frequency to those of its W -Master. To adjust carrier frequency and 898 
compensate aging and thermal drifts, the W -Devices shall measure the frequency deviation during reception 899 
of each Downlink. This devia tion is used by the W -Device for recalibration of its carrier frequency before 900 
each transmission.  901 
If a W -Device is waiting on pairing request from a W -Master longer than two minutes on the configuration 902 
channel, it shall start to sweep its carrier frequenc y in frequency steps of +/ - 25 kHz. Each frequency step 903 
is to be used four times before the next step. The maximum deviation of the sweep is +/ - 250 kHz.  904 
 W-Master Carrier frequency calibration  905 
The carrier frequencies of a W -Master should be calibrated to t he defined carrier frequency accuracy during 906 
manufacturing.  907 
 Modulation  908 
IO-Link Wireless  uses binary Gaussian frequency shift keying (GFSK) modulation scheme with a Gaussian 909 
filter bandwidth bit period product BT=0.5. The modulation index shall be 0.5.  (a modulation index value 910 
between 0.45 and 0.55 is tolerable).  911 
 912 
Figure 20 Base technology and modulation  913 
 914 
Figure 20 shows an unmodulated carrier frequency and the binary frequency modulated signal. A binary 915 
one shall be represented by a positive frequency deviation, and a binary zero shall be represented b y a 916 
negative frequency deviation . 917

© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 41 of 330  
  
The frequency transitions are non -linear (red circle in Figure 20 ) and cause interfering harmonics. A 918 
Gaussian filter reduces this impact. The entire modulation mechanism is named Gaussian Frequency Shift 919 
Keying (GFSK).  920 
 Transmission power  921 
The transmission power shall meet FCC  15.247 and EN  300 328 for the use of the 2 ,4 GHz ISM frequency 922 
band. For this reason, the maximum transmission power of a W -Master or W -Device should not exceed a 923 
total of 10 mW. If at a W -Master more than one W-Track  is used, all W-Tracks  are sharing the 10 mW. 924 
Thereby the antenna gain shall be ta ken in account.  925 
The output power shall be controlled by setting the attribute TransmitPower.  926 
The transmission power shall be sufficiently high in order to ensure the range and reliability requirements.  927 
The one -track W -Master shall be capable to transmit with a power of at least +6 dBm.  928 
multi -track W -Masters shall be capable to transmit with a power of at least 0 dBm per W-Track . 929 
The W -Device shall be capable to transmit with a power of at least +4 dBm.  930 
 Antenna  931 
If radio regulations (see 5.2.7 ) are met, a W -Master or W -Device can use internal or external antennas. If 932 
an antenna with direction characteristic is used, also the maximum transmission power of ≤ 10 dBm EIRP 933 
shall be observed for any direction.  934 
In order to ensure  the range and reliability requirements, a high antenna efficiency is preferable.  935 
 IO-Link Wireless r eceiver sensitivity  936 
The minimal radio  sensitivity on the W -Master  and W -Device side  shall be at least -94 dBm. For details see 937 
Annex K . 938

in Table 1 and Table 2 shall be fulfilled . 941

 Downlink and Uplink  CRC 1008 
CRC are necessary to avoid reception of a wrong message as a right one. The Pre-Downlink packet part  1009 
octets , Downlink - and Uplink  packet  has a CRC at the end to check its consistence after wireless 1010 
transmission. The Pre-Downlink packet part  CRC has a lengt h of 16 Bit. The Full -Downlink packet and all 1011 
Uplink  packet s have a CRC length of 32 Bit. To get the same probability of a correct message for Uplinks 1012 
and the Full -Downlink  packet  they need a longer CRC due to of their data length.  1013

5.4 W-Sub-cycle  1073 
 General  1074 
The general concept of the W -Cycle and the W -Sub-cycles is specified in Figure 17. The following 1075 
subclauses explain definitions for packets within a W-Frame .  1076 
 1077 
 W-Sub-cycle structure  1078 
A W-Sub-cycle describes a time frame with a duration of 1 ,664 ms , because the minimum cycle time shall 1079 
be shorter  than 5 ms. For this purpose,  the W-Sub-cycle has a length of 1 ,664 ms => 3 x cycle < 5 ms.  In 1080 
a W -Sub-cycle a complete communication exchange between a W -Master and its W -Devices is organized 1081 
(see  Figure 32). The detailed encoding of W -Messages within W -Sub-cycles are described in Annex D . 1082 
 1083 
The first part of a W -Sub-cycle is a control interval of 208 µs. In this interva l, the carrier frequency and 1084 
transceiver mode are adjusted. After that,  the so -called "Downlink" starts. The Downlink has a duration of 1085 
416 µs and can contain individual W -Message (s) for each W -Device, e.g.,  W-Device in Slot_N 2 in Figure 1086 
32. 1087

After the control interval during which the transceivers of the W -Master switches from "tr ansmit" ( Tx) to 1090 
"receive" ( Rx) and of the W -Device vice versa, the Uplink  transmissions  with a total duration of 832 µs 1091 
starts. In the “Uplink” each W -Device has its own time slot to response , e.g.,  Slot_N 2 for W -Device 2 in 1092 
Figure 32. 1093 
Between sequentially Uplink slots, a guard interval with a duration of 8 µs is placed. At the beginning of the 1094 
guard interval the previous W -Device stops sending, while the following W -Device starts sending at the end 1095 
of the guard interval. The guard interval is required for the W -Master to recover.  1096 
A W -Device can use two kinds of slots in an Uplink with different duration, Single Slots (SSlot) with 96 µs 1097 
(see  Figure 30) or Double Slot s (DSlot) with a length 200 µs (see Figure 31). Only by using SSlots, the 1098 
maximum number of 8 W -Devices per W-Track  can be achieved. DSlots shall always start at an even slot 1099 
number. If in a W-Track  an odd number of SSlots is used one SSlot cannot be used e.g.,  SSlot 5 in Figure 1100

 Regular W-Frame  1104 
Figure 34 shows the structure of a regular W-Frame , which is used for cyclic transmission of IO -Link 1105

W-Messages in its Downlink packet  addressed to dedicated W -Devices.  1107 
The first part of the Downlink  packet , the so -called Pre -Downlink, is integral part of the full Downlink  packet  1108 
for regular W-Frame s. It contains the acknowledgments and two octets  payl oad. Pre -Downlink  part  has its 1109 
own 16 bit CRC signature. Low energy W -Devices may reduce their receiver activity time by only receiving 1110 
the Pre -Downlink part instead of the full Downlink  packet .  1111 
All other W -Devices of the W-Track  shall receive the entire Downlink packet  and the 32 bit CRC signature 1112 
at the end.  1113

5.5 Medium Access Control (MAC)  1123 
 General  1124 
This clause describes the relevant definitions for media access on both, W -Master and W -Devices, which 1125 
shall comply with the requirements described below.  1126 
W-Devices and W -Master shal l operate in synchronous manner in frequency and time domain. Therefore, 1127 
the synchronization of the W -Devices on a W -Master is necessary. Initial synchronization of the W -Device 1128 
on its W -Master takes place during the pairing process. A paired W -Device resy nchronizes its timing on 1129 
each successful reception of the Downlink. The W -Devices calculates the next Downlink transmission time 1130 
and adds a window of uncertainty of 4 µs (DGUARD/2) to control its receiver activity.  1131 
W-Devices after a longer inactivity peri od might lose  clock synchronization with their W -Master. In this case 1132 
it is required to increase the uncertainty window of the W -Device, thus increasing the receiver on -time.  1133 
The use of orthogonal frequency channel hopping sequences by the W -Masters and t heir associated W -1134 
Devices allows operational coexistence of overlapping IO-Link Wireless  systems. The W -Master creates 1135 
the hopping sequences. For increasing capability of W-Coexistence, the frequency channel hopping 1136 
sequences can be adapted to environment using Blocklisting . During the pairing and configuration 1137 
processes, the W -Master downloads these hopping sequences into the unpaired W -Devices.  1138 
 Frequency channels  1139 
The carrier frequencies fn are defined by the frequency channel number n using Equation 2 1140 
MHznffn 10 +=
 
Equation 2 Carrier frequencies  1141 
where , 1142 
f0 = 2400 MHz  1143 
n = 3 to 78  for cyclic data exchange (see 4.5.2.4 ) and n = 1 and 80 for configuration (see 4.5.2.3 ). 1144 
The minimum spectral distance between the W-Tracks  of a W -Master is 3 MHz. Default Hopping Table is 1145 
according to HT01 . 1146 
IO-Link Wireless  defines the frequency channel hopping table HT01. HT01 omits the frequency channels 1147 
f1-2 and f 79-80. The f requency channels f 1 and f 80 are reserved for configuration (see 5.5.4 ). The frequency 1148 
channels f2 and f79 are always blocklisted  (see Table 209). Hopping table HT01 is organized in rows and 1149 
columns. In a column, all frequency channels used by a W -Master and its W -Devices within a W -Sub-cycle 1150 
are liste d. In a row, the sequence of frequency channels used by a W-Track  of a W -Master and its W -1151 
Devices is listed. HT01 additional allows blocklisting  of each 1 MHz frequency channel (see  5.5.5 ). 1152 
 1153 
The frequency hopping sequence of all W-Tracks  of overlapping W -Masters shall be orthogonal to avoid 1154 
transmission collisions within a W -Master. Therefore, in a cell with three W -Masters, the probability of 1155 
collisions by chance i s sufficiently low.   1156 
Each W-Track  of an IO-Link W -Master shall keep the hopping tables of all other W-Tracks  of this W -Master, 1157 
which enables a ( e.g.,  roaming) W-Track  to pair a W -Device to another target W-Track  of this W -Master.  1158 
Therefore,  the target W-Track  can stay in cyclic mode without loss of performance during pairing.  1159 
 1160 
The sequence of frequency channels in HT01 is determined by the HT01 parameters listed in Table 7. 1161

The Hopping Sequence is calculated in the IO -Link Wireless Master according to the following algorithm:  1166

 Alternative Hopping algorithms  1174

reserved  for future use.  1176 
 Configuration Frequencies  1177 
The frequencies f 1 and f 80 (i.e.,  2401 MHz and 2480 MHz) are exclusively reserved for configuration 1178 
channels. They shall be used in an alternating manner to reduce frequency related interferences. The 1179 
configuration frequencies cannot be blocklisted . Clause J.3 describes their utilization in detail.  1180 
 Blocklisting  1181 
In order to mitigate interference from or to other devices in the 2,4 GHz ISM band affected frequency 1182 
channels can be omitted by appending the m to the blocklist  (see Table 210). It should be taken in to account, 1183 
that the reduction of available frequency channels can compromise latency in a non-deterministic manner.  1184 
See clause H.2 for calculation rules and examples.  1185 
 Wireless Quality  1186 
5.5.6.1  Link Quality Indication  (LQI_M, LQI_D)  1187 
Link Quality Indication is a se rvice for evaluation of the functionality and reliability of the IO -Link Wireless 1188 
System in certain application environments. The LQI shall be calculated on the W -Master (LQI_M) as well 1189 
as on the W -Device (LQI_D) Physical Layer.  This service should be used  during commissioning or 1190 
significant changes during the running period of the IO -Link Wireless System. Optional it can be used during 1191 
operating mode of the IO -Link Wireless system for monitoring the wireless environment regarding reliability.  1192 
To analyze co nnection quality independent from RSSI the Link Quality Indication shall be evaluated on 1193 
each W -Port. Therefore, the Packet Error Ratio (PER) per sub cycle (PERSubCycle) and MaxRetry are 1194 
used to calculate the Link Quality Indication with  1195

Figure 36 LQI vs PERSubCycle for different MaxRetry  1219 
NOTE1: In general, the Link Quality Indication is only an estimate of the expected reliability, rather than a 1220 
precise measurement.  1221 
In certain situations,  there may be a discrepancy between the reliability estimated by the Link Quality 1222 
Indication and the reliability actually achieved.  1223 
For example, for a frequency -selective but almost static radio channel between the W -Master and the W -1224 
Device (such as multip ath propagation due to many reflections and almost no moving objects in the 1225 
environment), the Link Quality Indication may lead to a worse estimate of reliability than can actually be 1226 
achieved.  1227 
5.5.6.2  Received Signal Strength Indication (RSSI_M, RSSI_D)  1228 
The RSSI i s an estimated measure of power level that a W -Master (RSSI_M) or W -Device (RSSI_D) is 1229 
receiving from its counterpart. It is interpreted as an indication of arriving signal  power strength at the 1230 
antenna. The Values of the RSSI can be used by application as  a user information.  1231 
NOTE  that not all radio transceivers supports the RSSI. See clause C.4.8  for definition.  1232

5.7.1.2  Configuration sequence and retry handling for Scan.  1860 
Figure 38 describes the sequence for a discovery procedure. The W -Master sends a Scan Request 1861 
Downlink (see Figure 121) on each configuration W -Sub-cycle with a continuously incremented request 1862 
number (Request _N).  1863

(see Figure 135) on W-Sub-cycle  X. X is calculated with  Equation 6. 1865

The W -Master acknowledges the reception of Scan Response by setting the corresponding ack -bit in the 1886 
next Scan Request. In order to avoid ambiguity, the next Scan Request includes also the received UniqueID 1887 
of the W -Device (see Figure 128). If a W -Device does not receive an ACK  for its scan response it shall not 1888 
use the next configuration W -Sub-cycle for retry transmission because this could cause further packet 1889 
coll isions. The retry shall be generated after a random number (in the range of 2…31) of configuration W -1890 
Sub-cycles in stead.  1891

===PAGE 83===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 83 of 330  
  
STATE NAME  STATE DESCRIPTION  
CyclicMode_2  Cyclic W -Frame exchange between W -Master and W -Devices in 
CyclicMode:  
The state performs the creation of the W -Frames, starting with the 
transmission of DLin k and the handling of ULinks.  
After each W -Frame (all ULinks processed) this state changes the radio 
frequency to the next frequency specified by the frequency hopping table.  
RoamingMode_3  Cyclic W -Frame exchange between W -Master and W -Devices in 
RoamingM ode:  
Perform the creation of each W -Frame, starting with the transmission of 
DLink and the reception of ULinks.  
After each W -Frame (all ULinks processed) this state changes the radio 
frequency to the next frequency given by the frequency hopping table.  
At every 5th W -Sub-cycle, the frequency given by the hopping table is 
overwritten by one of the configuration frequencies in an alternating 
sequence.  
NOTE : Due to the Scan Request Downlinks every 5th W -Sub-cycle, the 
cyclic data channel availability  at a cycle time of 5 ms might be affected. 
Therefore, it is recommended to use W -Devices with a W -Cycle of 
minimum 10 ms within a roaming W-Track.  
SM: Handle_SubCycle_4  This Submachine cyclically transmits the W -Frames (DLink payload and 
processing of all ULinks). It is used by State „CyclicMode_2“ and 
“RoamingMode_3“, dependent on PL_SetMode(Cyclic or Roaming), see 
T3 and T4. Furthermore, this state generates a trigger to handle every 5th 
Frame for the Modes SCAN, PAIRING and ROAMING, sel ected by 
service PL_SetMode in state „Start_next_Frame_5“.  
SM: Start_next_Frame_5  This state loads the data from MH (reported via service PL_Transfer) to 
the payload data of the DLink (see Figure 126). If MH has no data to send 
(PL_Transfer hasn‘t be called), set the payload data to 0 (DLink without 
data).  
Get the next frequency which shall be used for the following DLink from 
the frequency table.  
SM: SubCycle_Count_6  This state is used to trigger every 5th W -Frame for the Modes SCAN, 
PAIRING and ROAMING.  
SM: SendDownlink_7  Sending of the Regular Downlink over the air on the frequency selected in 
state “Start_next_Frame_5”.  
SM:Check_W -
Device_Configur ed_8  
This state checks, if the actual Slot (W -Device) is configured.  
SM: WaitULink_9  Waiting for the reception of the actual Uplink  transmission  until the Uplink  
packet  has been received or the Timer T TUlink exceeds (See Figure 131 
and Figure 132). 
NOTE : The CRC 32 calculation of a cyclic ULink  considers the  
DeviceDistinguishingID of this slot (_X) (see B.7 Pre-set value for cyclic 
ULink ). 
SM: Check_IMA_10  This state handles the IMA supervision for the actual Slot, since the W -
Device is synchronized but Uplink  packet  has not been received.  
SM: Check_Mode_11  This state is called every 5th W -Sub-cycle for the Modes SCAN, PAIRING 
and ROAMING to select the DLink which shall be sent on a configuration 
frequency.  
SM: Mode_Scan_12  This submachine handles the DLink and ULinks for SCAN mode.  
SM: SendScanRequest_13  This state handles the generation of the Scan Request Downlink (See 
B.3.2  Scan Downlink)  
SM: WaitOnResponse_14  This state handles the reception of the Scan response Uplinks (up to four 
Scan response s are possible,  
see B.5.2 . Scan Response Uplink  packet ) 
SM: Mode_Pairing_15  This substate machine handles the Pairing mode.  
SM: Check_Pair_State_16  This state handles the generation of the next Pairing downlink depending 
on the Pairing “STATE“

T2 1 1 Activation by System Management through 
PL_SetSlotConfig(ParameterList).  
PL_SetSlotConfig prepares the corresponding Slot “_X” given in slot 
number (Slot_N) for a proper connection in the following way:  
Slot_N:  
Points to the receive time within the TDMA sl ot assignment ( see 
Figure 32 and Figure 33) 
UniqueID:  
The last 4 octets of the UniqueID (Device Distinguishing ID) is used as 
final XOR of the CRC32 checksum (see B.7.: Final XOR of a regula r 
ULink)  
SlotType:  
Defines the length of the ULink (see Figure 30 and Figure 31) to setup 
the transceiver receive length.  
IMATime:  
Defines the number of W -Sub-cycles to observe the presence of the 
W-Device  (see clause C.4.4.2  for encoding).  
Set SlotCfg_X = OK.  
PL_SetMode shall return PARAMETER_CONFLICT if the SlotType is 
DSlot and Slot_N not even.

DL-mode handler .

T57 5 5 Activation by Data Link Command Handler through PL_CmdTrig.  
Command:  
Case WAKE_UP_TIME:  
Invoke PL_WakeUpTime(WakeUpCountdown, Slot_N)  
Case W_DEVICE_AWAKE:  
Indicates low energy W -Device sent IMA at WakeUpTime, if all low 
energy W -Devices sent an IMA then return PL_CmdTrig.cnf(JUMP)  
Case W_DEVICE_NOT_AWAKE:  
Indicates low energy W -Device did not send  IMA at WakeUpTime, 
return PL_CmdTrig.cnf(WAKE_UP_ABORT) and 
PL_AHTStatus(WAKE_UP_ABORT)  
Case JUMP:  
Switch to new hopping table , starting with  HOP-1 frequency, invoke 
PL_AHTStatus(JUMP_SUCCESS)

 PL W -Device state machine  1986 
5.7.3.1  General  1987 
Figure 47 shows the main state machine of the W -Device Physical Layer. The state machine handles the 1988 
initial initialization of the W -Device, Pairing, Unpairing and Cyclic operation. Depending on the pa iring 1989 
request, the W -Device is either configured as a Roaming or as a Cyclic W -Device. The main difference 1990 
between two modes is the reaction on a communication Sync_lost event. In case the W -Device is configured 1991 
for roaming, it goes in to the Unpaired_2 st ate immediately. Otherwise, it goes to the Paired_4 state and 1992 
waits until W -Master comes back in range or pairing by button is activated by Application Layer.  1993

5.7.3.2  DLink processing in the W -Device via PL  2004 
On the reception of each DLink, the PL shall parse the DLink payload to collect all W -Messages directed to 2005 
this addressed W -Device or any broadcast Master -Command. The collected W -Messages (ControlOctet s 2006 
included) shall be sen t to the DL -A message handler via Transfer.ind. If there are one or more W -Messages 2007 
for the current  W-Device  received, the PL shall generate  an acknowledgement, which shall be automatically 2008 
transmitted in the following uplink  packet . If Transfer.req was not invoked, then the payload shall be set to 2009 
zero (dummy Uplink  packet ). 2010 
 2011 

===PAGE 98===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 98 of 330  
  
6 Data Link Layer (DL -A) 2012 
6.1 General  2013 
The Data Link Layers are concerned w ith the delivery of messages between a W -Master and a W -Device.  2014 
A set of DL -services is available to the Application Layer (AL) for the exchange of Process Data (PD) and 2015 
Event or ISDU data. Another set of DL -services is available to system management (SM) for the retrieval 2016 
of W-Device  identification parameters and the setting of state machines within the DL. The DL uses PL -2017 
Services for controlling the Physical Layer (PL). The DL takes care of the error detection of messages 2018 
(whether internal or reported fro m the PL) and the appropriate remedial measures ( e.g.,  retry).  2019 
The Data Link Layers are structured due to the nature of the data categories into Process Data handlers 2020 
and Event / ISDU handlers which are in turn using a Message handler to deal with the requ ested 2021 
transmission of messages. Each handler comprises its own state machine.  2022 
The Data Link Layer is subdivided in a DL -A section with its own internal services and a DL -B section with 2023 
the external services.  2024 
The DL uses additional internal administrative calls between the handlers which are defined in the "internal 2025 
items" section of the associated state -transition tables.  2026 
6.2 General (W -Master)  2027 
 General  2028 
Figure 99 shows an overview of the structure and the services of the W -Master's Data Link Layer.  2029 
 W-Track and W -Device -Mapper (TDMapper)  2030 
The W-Track and W -Device -Mapper  is used to assign a W -Port to a s pecific W-Track number (Track_N) 2031 
and Slot number (Slot_N). Each Slot_N represents a W -Device, whereupon the W -Device communicates 2032 
via this Slot to the W -Master. The Slot_N is transmitted during pairing to the selected W -Device. The 2033 
configuration of the TDM apper is done by SM_SetPortConfig service (via DL_TDConfig). This mapping 2034 
table enables a flexible assignment of W -Devices without changing of the W -Port, e.g.,  distribution of W -2035 
Devices within the W-Tracks . 2036

 UpLinkAck (W -Device)  2173 
The service UpLinkAck is only available on the W -Device. The service triggers the appropriate handler (PD 2174 
handler, EV handler, or ISDU handler) to provide data for the next Uplink message. With the Acknowledge 2175 
from the last downlink each handler has to decide, if new data have to be sent , or the  last data have to be 2176 
retransmitted. The parameters of the service are listed in Table 41 2177

6.5 Acknowledgments (DownLinkAck and UpLinkAck)  2310 
W-Devices acknowledge correct reception of the downlink message within their uplink messages. Within 2311 
the next downlink, the W -Master acknowledges correct reception of the last uplink messages to each W -2312 
Device. I n case of negative acknowledgments, both the W -Master and W -Devices use this information to 2313 
initiate transmission retries.  2314

Table 45 State transition tables of the W -Master Message handler  2330 
STATE NAME  STATE DESCRIPTION  
Inactive_0  Waiting for activation by W -Master DL -mode handler through 
MH_Conf_ACTIVE (see Figure 52). Set RemainingLength to DLink -Payload 
(37 Octet).  
Idle_1  Waiting for trigger PL_Transfer.ind service indication. The PL_Transfer 
service delivers the slot number (0 up to 7) and further parameters within a 
W-Sub-cycle, which represents a W -Device at this Slot.  
Process_ULink_2  Check message for valid ULink Control Octets. For message encoding of 
the ULink Control Octet see Figure 123, ULink Control Octet  
Check_last_DLink_3  Check if data h ave been sent for this W -Device / Slot_N in last downlink  
Get_CMD_4  The Message handler starts to compile the message for the next DLink 
using the DownLinkAck service to acquire a MasterCommand from the 
Command handler . The Message handler waits on the MCmd.req service 
and then changes to state GetPDOut_5.  
GetPDOut_5  The Message handler uses the DownLinkAck service to acquire PDOut data 
from the PDOut handler. The Message handler waits for the PDOutMsg .req 
service to complement an already acquired MCmd.  
GetEvent_ 6 The Message handler uses the DownLinkAck service to acquir e a possible 
Event response from the Event handler. The Message handler waits on the 
EventMsg .rsp service to complement the already acquired PDOut / MCmd.  
GetISDU_ 7 The Message handler uses the DownLinkAck service to acquire ISDU from 
the ISDU handler. The Message handler waits on the ISDUMsg .req service 
to complement the already acquired PD / MCmd / Event data.

INTERNAL ITEMS  TYPE  DEFINITION   
RemainingLength  Variable  Remaining length in DLink payload.  
W-FRAME_COMPLETE  Variable  Marks the W -Sub-cycle as completed, if all ULinks have been 
processed.  
SlotCounter  Variable  Counter to compile the acyclic data for all Slots / W-Device s 
RetryCounter_X  Variable  Counter for not acknowledged DLinks.  
IMACounter_X  Variable  Counter to observe ULink -IMA-frames which shall be sent by W -
Device_X.  
DL_Data_sent_X  Variable  Variable to store the information, that data have been sent in last 
DLink for the corresponding Slot / W -Device.  
ACK_Buf_X  Variable  Store Acknowledge for slot number X  
PreDLSet  Bool  Marks if the PreDownlink will be used or not.  
ResetCounter_X  Variable  Marks if a DeviceOperate MasterCommand has been sent and 
cycle counters shall be reset.  
SubCycleCounterOut_X  Variable  Counter for W -Sub-Cycles of the WMasterCycleTimeOut  
SubCycleCounterIn_X  Variable  Counter for W -Sub-Cycles of the WMasterCycleTimeIn  
 2333 
NOTE  : _X marks the variables which individual for every Slotnumber. The range of _X is slot number 0 to 2334

 2736 
7.2 DL-mode handler  2737 
 General  2738 
The W -Master DL -mode handler is responsible to establish communication using services of the Physical 2739 
Layer (PL) and internal administrative calls to control and monitor the states of other handlers.  2740 
The W -Device DL -mode handler receives MasterCommands to synchronize with the W -Master DL -mode 2741 
handler states STARTUP, PREOPERATE, OPERATE and manages  the activation and deactivation of 2742 
handlers as appropriate.  2743 

===PAGE 127===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 127 of 330  
  
 State machine of the W -Master DL -mode handler  2744 
 2745 
After reception of the service DL_SetMode(STARTUP) from system management, the W -Master waits for 2746 
synchronization with the W -Device.  2747 
The purpose of state "Startup_2" is to check a W -Device's identity in state "PreOperate_3", the W -Master 2748 
may assign parameters to the W -Device using ISDUs. Cyclic exchange of Process Data is performed in 2749 
state "Operate". Within this state additional data such as Commands, Events and ISDUs can be transmitted.  2750 
 2751 
Figure 52 State Machine of the  W-Master DL -mode handler  2752 
 2753 
Idle_0
EstablishCom_1
Preoperate_3
StartUp_2
/initialization
Operate_4
PL_State_COMLOST /T12
DL_SetMode_OPERATE /T5
DL_SetMode_STARTUP /T7

INTERNAL ITEMS  TYPE  DEFINITION   
PD_Out _Status  Variable  Indicate if PDOut is valid or invalid  
0 = PDOUT_INVALID  
1 = PDOUT_VALID  
cycle_count  Variable  Counting variable for W -Sub-cycles

NO = Segment not sent and acknowledged  
YES = Segment sent and acknowledged

0 = PDOUT_INCOMPLETE  
1 = PDOUT_COMPLETE

of first data packet and activation of PDCycle . 2790

 General structure and encoding of ISDUs  2865 
The encoding of ISDU data (I -Service -octet and data) delivered by the ISDU handler shall be implemented 2866 
equal to IO -Link (see A.5 in [1]), with the exception of the definition of the nibble "I -Service".  2867 
This specification shall only support the I -Service Read Request or Write Request with 16 -bit Index and 2868 
Subindex, as defined in Table 74. 2869

7.6 Event handler  2899 
 General  2900 
An Event transfers a status or an error information.  2901 
The Event request is sent from W -Device to  W-Master . It is then processed by the  W-Master  and an Event 2902 
Confirmation is sent back to the  W-Device . Events are serviced one by one, so further Event requests are 2903 
ignored until the current Event has been serviced and conf irmed.  2904 
The general structure and coding of Events is specified in Annex A.6.in [1] 2905 
EventCodes are specified in Annex D  2906 
 2907

 2932 
7.7 Transmission of segmented data and retry handling  2933 
 General  2934 
Data which can‘t be sent in one message have to be transmitted within a number of segments. To achieve 2935 
a proper mechanism particularly in combination with possible retransmits, each DL -B handler (Process Data 2936 
handler, ISDU handler and Event handler) shall generate its own Flow Control considering the acknowledge 2937 
of the last sent W -Message.  2938 
 Transmission of segmented data 2939 
The transmission of segmented data is possible for Process Data ( e.g.,  for distribution of process data 2940 
within a W -Cycle, see Figure 55, Events and ISDU Data.  2941 
The ULink and DLink Control Octets accommodates a counter (=FlowControl). FlowControl is controlling 2942 
the segmented data flow by counting the seque nces necessary to transmit segmented data (see Table 79). 2943 
 2944 
• A segment begins with FlowControl = START.  2945 
• All following segments use FlowControl = COUNT to number each  data segment. In case of a retry 2946 
during COUNT, take account to 7.7.3 . 2947 
• The transmission of the last segment differs between Process Data and Event - or ISDU -data:  2948 
a. Last segment for Process Data Out (transmitted via DLink):  2949 
To indicate a complete data transmission to W -Device set FlowControl = EOS immediately.  2950 

===PAGE 157===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 157 of 330  
  
b. Last segment for Process Data In (transmitted via ULink):  2951 
To indicate a complete data transmission to W -Master set FlowControl = PDataLength  2952 
(see Table 79 column Process Data In)  2953 
c. Last segment for acyclic Event - and ISDU -data (transmitted via DLink o r ULink):  2954 
To indicate a complete data transmission, the Event handler or ISDU -handler  shall send a 2955 
separate W -Message with FlowControl = EOS and without data to achieve data consistency 2956 
due to retransmits.  2957 
NOTE  1: 2958 
A MasterCommand as well as an Event acknowledge does not  need segmentation, since this W -Messages 2959 
are transmitted without data (see Table 46). 2960 
 2961 
Table 79 Flow Control for segmented data  2962 
FlowControl  
(FC)  
FlowControl  
(FC)  
Definition  
0x00 to 0x07  COUNT  
Counter within a data segment. Increments beginning with 1 after a START. Jumps 
back from 7 to 0 in case of an overflow.  
0x08  START  
Start of a segment, i.e., start of an ISDU - request or a response. For the start of a 
request, any previously incomplete service may be rejected.  
0x09  EOS 
End of Segment indicates a completed transmission. (Event - or ISDU: separate EOS 
within next DLink)  
0x0A  ABORT  
Abort entire service. Th e W-Master responds by rejecting received response data.  
The W-Device responds by rejecting received request data and may generate an abort.   
Definition for  
DLink  Control Octet  
See NOTE  2 
Definition for  
ULink  Control Octet  
See NOTE  3 
Process Data In  
(data transmission 
complete)  
Event - or ISDU data  
(separate EOS within next 
ULink)  
0x0B  Unused  PDataLength = 1 octet  DataLength = 1 octet  
0x0C…0x17  Unused  PDataLength = 2…13 octet  DataLength = 2…13 octet  
0x18  Unused  PDataLength = 14 octet  DataLength = 14 octet  
0x19 to 0x1F  Reserved  Reserved  Reserved  
 2963 
NOTE  2: 2964 
The DLink Control Octet (see Figure 121) contains a separate field to transmit the length of data. Therefore, 2965 
these values are unused.  2966 
 2967 
NOTE  3: 2968 
The ULink Control Octet (see Figure 123) is coded by only one octet (reduced overhead). Therefore, the 2969 
DataLength is coded within the Flow Control.  2970 
 2971 
Additionally see A.6. Example for DLink data transmission and A.7 Examples for ULink data transmission 2972 
for data transmi ssion examples.  2973 
 Retry -Handling  2974 
7.7.3.1  General  2975 
For an appropriate data transmission, the “Sender” shall retransmit its last W -Message, if the service 2976 
DownLinkAck or UpLinkAck delivered a negative Acknowledge (NACK  or not received message ) to the 2977 
corresponding hand ler (ProcessData -, Event -, Command -, or ISDU -handler ) see 5.6.2.9 . 2978 

===PAGE 158===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 158 of 330  
  
7.7.3.2  Retry handling in case of not segmented data  2979 
The corresponding handler shall retransmit its last  W-Message, depending on the remaining payload in the 2980 
DLink or ULink (see service 6.4.4  and 6.4.5 .). 2981 
7.7.3.3  Retry handling in case of segmented data / Flow Control.  2982 
If the “Sender”  does not  receiv e an ACK for its last sent W -Message,  it has to forward NACK to the layer 2983 
above and  it has to resend the last data and the value of the last FlowCtrl.  2984 
If the “Receiver” thereupon gets new FlowCtrl = last FlowCtrl, it has to reject the last received data segment 2985 
and use the new received data segment instead. This behavior is essentially, since a W -Message with an 2986 
ACK could be corrupted ( e.g.,  on air), which leads in a NACK on the receiver side.  2987 
 2988 
NOTE : “Sender” or “Receiver” can be W -Master or W -Device  2989 
 2990 

===PAGE 159===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 159 of 330

This parameter contains the number of the related W -Port.  3257 
ControlCode  3258 
This parameter contains the qualifier status of the Process Data (PD).  3259 
Permitted values:  3260 
PDIN_ VALID (Input Process Data valid)  3261

9. System management (SM)  3388 
9.1 General  3389 
The system management (SM) services are used for the coordinated startup and configuration of the 3390 
possible operational modes within the W -Master and the corresponding W -Devices. Since the diffe rence 3391 
between the SM of the W -Master and the W -Device is significant, the structure of this clause separates the 3392 
services and protocols of W -Master and W -Device.  3393 
 3394 
NOTE: S ome of the functionality described in this clause is implemented within the underlyin g PL, such as 3395 
the handling of the W-Message s. 3396 
 3397 
The following subclauses describe the possible operational modes and associated procedures.  3398 
9.2 Modes  3399 
 Service Mode  3400 
When a W-Track  is configured to operate in ServiceMode, the frequency hopping table also utilizes the 3401 
configuration frequency channels. The ServiceMode can be configured as Scan Mode, Pairing Mode or 3402 
Roaming Mode. Scan and Pairing Mode are terminating automatically afte r the intended procedure is 3403 
completed. Roaming Mode stays permanently active and a discovery procedure is regularly carried out by 3404 
issuing “Scan Request” messages on the configuration channels. This is required for the “Handover 3405 
Connect” procedure.  3406 
 3407 
 Cyclic  Mode  3408 
9.2.2.1  General  3409 
In Cyclic Mode, the W -Master W-Track  communicates with the W -Device via the assigned data channel by 3410 
utilizing the frequency hopping table without configuration frequencies. This mode is utilized with fixed W -3411 
Devices.  3412 
After successful pairing of all W -Devices for a W-Track , the W -Master can  switch via SM_SetTrackMode 3413 
the mode from ServiceMode to Cyclic Mode. On the W -Device, the Cyclic Mode is immediately entered 3414 
after successful sending of the final "Pairing Negotiation Response". Scan, Pairing and Roaming is no 3415 
longer possible on this W-Track in this mode.  3416 
9.2.2.2  IMATime monitoring  3417 
The IMATime is continuously supervised within the PL. The IMATime is transferred within the W-Parameter  3418 
to the W -Device during the STARTUP procedure via SM_SetPortConfig.  3419 
The monitoring is started after the W -Device is synchronized. In case of an IMATimeout a COMLOST and 3420 
an IOLW_IMATimeout event (see clause  D.2) will be generated towards the application.  3421 
When an offered IMATime ( e.g.,  from PDCT) is rejected by the W -Device, this is indicated via an ISDU 3422 
ErrorType ( e.g.,  PAR_VALOUTOFRNG) towards the application. In this case, the W -Master executes a 3423 
DL_Read(IMATime) and starts monitoring using the value from the W -Device until the application changes 3424 
this setting.  3425 
The allowed range of the IMATime shall be described in the IODD of the W -Device.  3426 
 3427 

===PAGE 180===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 180 of 330

 3443 
9.3 System management of the W -Master  3444 
 Overview  3445 
The W -Master SM  3446 
• Establishes the required communication protocol revision . 3447 
• Checks the W -Device compatibility (actual W -Device identifications match expected values) . 3448 
• Adjusts adequate cycle times . 3449 
• Computes the frequency hopping tables . 3450 
• Assigns W -Port numbers to the wireless communication relations.  3451 
For this it uses the following services shown in Figure 99. 3452 
• SM_SetMasterConfig  sets the common configuration of the W -Master for all W-Tracks . 3453 
• SM_SetTrackMode sets the mode of a W-Track . 3454 
• SM_GetTrackMode gets the mode of a W-Track . 3455 
• SM_TrackScanEnd indicates the end of the scan mode.  3456 
• SM_TrackScanResult reports a new unpaired W -Device within the W-Track ’s proximity to the 3457 
application.  3458 
• SM_SetPortConfig transfers the necessary parameters (configuration data) from Configuration 3459 
Management (CM) to System Management (SM). The communication is then started implicitly.  3460 
• SM_PortMode reports the result of the setup back to CM, in case of negative result via corresponding 3461 
"errors", such as misma tching revisions and incompatible W -Devices.  3462 
• SM_GetPortConfig reads the actual and effective parameters.  3463 
• SM_Operate switches the ports into the "OPERATE" mode.  3464 
• SM_GetPortQuality delivers the quality of the port connection.  3465 
• SM_PortPairing handle s the pairing process.  3466 
 3467 
The Configuration Manager in a W -Master consists of Master Configuration Manager, W-Track 3468 
Configuration Manager and Port Configuration Manager. During initialization, the W -Master’s Configuration 3469 
management (CM) first reads the con figuration for the W -Master (MasterID, Block List). In the next step, the 3470 
W-Master Configuration will be applied,  and the radios will be switched on by SM_SetMasterConfig and the 3471 
W-Master starts sending Downlinks on the W-Track  specific frequency channels u ntil it gets an answer from 3472 
a W -Device in the corresponding uplink slot. The W -Devices are then synchronized,  and the W -Master 3473 
application may call the DL service DL_SetMode (STARTUP) to create the required instances of the Master 3474 
DL-mode handler.  3475 
 3476 
Figure 79 demonstrates the actions between the layers W -Master application (W -Master App), 3477 
Configuration Management (CM), System Management (SM), Data Link (DL) and Applicat ion Layer (AL) for 3478 
the startup use case of a particular port . 3479 
 3480 
This particular use case is characterized by the following statements:  3481 
• The W -Device for the available configuration is connected and inspection is successful  3482 
• The W -Device uses the correct RevisionID according to this specification  3483 

===PAGE 181===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 181 of 330  
  
• The configured InspectionLevel is "type compatible" (SerialNumber is read out of the W -Device, but 3484 
not checked).  3485 
 3486

SM_GetPortConfig  Get configuration of a virtual W-Port  R 
SM_PortPairing  Pair W -Device to W -Master  R 
SM_PortMode  Reports the mode of a W-Port  I 
SM_GetPortQuality  Acquire quality of a W -Device connection  R 
SM_Operate  Activate a W-Port  R 
Key ( see 3.3.5 )  
I: Initiator of service  
R: Receiver (Responder) of service  
 3498
