Annex I  6624 
(normative)  6625 
How to get a certified product  6626 
I.1 General  6627 
In order to get a certified IO-Link Wireless  product, different testing and certification aspects shown in 6628 
clause I.2 and I.3 shall be considered:  6629 
I.2 Radio Certification  6630 
To satisfy the legal jurisdiction under which the  wireless equipment shall be used, the locally valid regulatory 6631 
compliance rules for wireless equipment shall be fulfilled. Currently relevant regulations are outlined in 6632 
more detail in Annex I  “Regulatory Compliance”.  6633 
I.3 IO-Link Certification  6634 
Compliance to the IO-Link Wireless  protocol defined in this standard shall be documented with a 6635 
manufacturer self -declaration and associated test reports for the specific product, containing the aspects 6636 
of both protocol conformity and performance conformity.  6637 
The requir ed testing procedures and recommended test lab services towards the testing references will 6638 
also be described in the separate IO-Link Wireless  test specification see [11] . 6639 

===PAGE 321===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 321 of 330  
  
Annex J  6640 
(normative)  6641 
Regulatory Compliance  6642 
J.1 General  6643 
This Annex I  provide  requirements for compliance of IO-Link Wireless  devices operating in the 2 ,4 GHz ISM 6644 
band with several regulatory standards. For operation in the United States, FCC 15.247 must be met (see 6645 
clause J.2). Different requirements apply in Europe , which can be met by complying with FCC 15.247 in 6646 
combination with harmonized standards EN 300 328 (see clause  J.3) and EN 300 440 (see clause  J.4). 6647 
 6648 
It is generally recommended to handle the regulatory standards in a similar approach as the Bluetooth low 6649 
Power Special Interest Group has outlined in  [5] ("Bluetooth Low Energy Regulatory Aspects") . 6650 
 6651 
Additionally, ETSI Guide EG 203 367 contains guidance information in assessing conformity against the 6652 
essential requirements of the Radio Equipmen t Directive 2014/53/EU (RED) for the combination of radio 6653 
and non -radio products as well as the integration of several radios into a single equipment.  6654 
 6655 
 6656 
NOTE: In this Annex I , the terms “devices” and "equipment" are used synonymously and refer to electroni cs with radios operating 6657 
according to the appropriate standard.  6658 
 6659 
J.2 Compliance with FCC 15.247  6660 
To comply with FCC 15.247, the manufacturer should declare IO-Link Wireless  equipment according to 6661 
FCC §15.247 -a2 as “ systems using digital modulation ”, where " the minimum 6 dB bandwidth shall be 6662 
at least 500 kHz ". This requires static testing at the relevant frequency channels (typically band edges and 6663 
center channel) while frequency hopping algorithms are not subject of compliance testing procedures, see  6664 
[7]. 6665 
 6666 
J.3 Compliance with ETSI EN 300 328  6667 
EN 300 328 is listed as a harmonized standard under the Radio Equipment Directive 2014/53/EU.  6668 
 6669 
To comply with EN 300 328, the manufa cturer should declare its IO -Link Wireless equipment as utilizing 6670 
"other types of Wide Band modulation " with "RF Output power is less than 10 dBm e.i.r.p." and being 6671 
a "non -adaptive equipment".  6672 
The limit of 10 dBm shall apply for any combination of power l evel and intended antenna assembly. If more 6673 
than one W-Track  (antenna) is used in a device, all W-Tracks  are sharing the 10 dBm. For example, 3 dBm 6674 
per W-Track  are permitted in case of five W-Tracks . 6675 
The required test suites shall  be carried out and compli ance declared for the relevant technical requirements 6676 
see [8]. 6677 
 6678 
J.4 Compliance with ETSI EN 300 440  6679 
EN 300 440 is listed as harmonized standard under the Radio Equipment  Directive 2014/53/EU.  6680 
 6681 
To comply with EN 300 440, the manufacturer should declare its IO -Link Wireless equipment as " Non-6682 
specific short -range device " restricted to a " Maximum radiated peak power (e.i.r.p.) " of " 10 mW e.i.r.p. " 6683 
The e.i.r.p. is defined as the " maximum radiated power of the transmitter and its antenna ", thus antenna 6684 
gains better than 0 dBi require an adequate power adjustment.  6685 
The required test suites must be carried out and compliance declared for the relevant technical 6686 
requirem ents see [9] and [10] . 6687 
 6688 

===PAGE 322===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 322 of 330  
  
Annex K  6689 
(informative)  6690 
 6691 
Radio field planning  6692 
K.1 General  6693 
The f ollowing section explains in a highly simplified and abbreviated form how to estimate the range and 6694 
reliability of an IOLW system. For simplification, some assumptions ( e.g.,  no interferers, etc.) are made.  6695 
K.2 Range and reliability examination  6696 
K.2.1  General  6697 
Typical ly, the sensitivity is specified as the minimum receive power required to achieve a certain bit error 6698 
probability (BEP), i.e.,  10 -3. In the following two figures Figure K. 1 and Figure K.2, the BEP as a function of 6699 
received power is exemplarily shown for two example chips, based  on theoretical assumptions.  6700 
Sensitivity EXAMPLE CHIP 1  6701 
BEP = 1*10 -3  => sensitivity level -91 dBm  6702 
 6703 
Figure K. 1 BEP over received power EXAMPLE CHIP 1 (based on measurements, interpolated).  6704 
Sensitivity EXAMPLE CHIP 2   6705 
BEP = 1*10 -3  => sensitivity level -94 dBm  6706 


===PAGE 323===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 323 of 330  
  
 6707 
Figure K.2 BEP over received power EXAMPLE CHIP 2 (theoretically derived fr om previous 6708 
measurements).  6709 
 6710 
For packet -based communication such as IO -Link Wireless, the BEP is usually not directly measured, but 6711 
it can be related to the Packet Error Probability (PEP), subject to certain conditions.  6712 
In a highly simplified form and under  the condition, that at least one -bit error within a packet results in a 6713 
packet error, the PEP can be calculated from the BEP as follows  6714 
𝑃𝐸𝑃 = 1 − (1 − 𝐵𝐸𝑃)𝑛_𝐵𝑖𝑡𝑠 , 6715 
whereby n_Bits is the number of bits within a packet. For example, for an IO -Link Wireless downlink packet 6716 
and BEP = 1*10 -3 = 0.001, this results in approx. PEP≈0.3 .  6717 
Thus, the sensitivity can be derived by a packet error measurement (PEP). It can be performed either, if 6718 
possible ( i.e. , accessible antenna connector), as a conducted meas urement ( i.e.,  in dBm) or as a radiated 6719 
(over -the-air) measurement ( i.e.,  dBm EIRP). However, for a final IO -Link Wireless product, the losses 6720 
caused by the antenna (antenna efficiency) and the matching circuit (including e.g.,  a “balun”) have to be 6721 
taken into account.  6722 
In general, IOLW does not prescribe a specific orientation of W -Devices and W -Masters in an application 6723 
environment. Thus, the later positioning or orientation of e.g.,  W-Devices is generally unknown for a 6724 
manufacturer, so that all conceivab le scenarios with regard to spatial directions of incidence and 6725 
polarizations must be taken into account in the test. This can be done in a relatively simple way using a 6726 
reverberation chamber (RC).  6727 
Based on the well -proven Friis transmission formula e.g., [12] ,[13] , the path -loss in free space results in 6728 
approximat ely the following attenuation for the given ranges and the 2 ,4 GHz band:  6729 
Friis -> Free space path loss:  6730 
       2 m  -46 dB  6731 
       4 m  -52 dB  6732 
       8 m  -58 dB  6733 
       10 m   -60 dB  6734 
       20 m   -66 dB  6735 
A negative sign means attenuation here.  6736 
As a worst -case estimation, a radio channel without direct line of sight (NLOS = Non -Line of Sight) is 6737 
assumed which is modelled or described as Rayleigh radio channel. It was shown in e.g. [14] , [15]  that a 6738 
obstructed or direct line of sight (OLOS or LOS) or path between a transmitter and a receiver can be 6739 
described e.g., as a Rician radio channel (Rice channel) Furthermore it was shown in e.g. [14] , [15]  that 6740 
deep fading, which can lead to interruptions or outages of a radio connection, occur much less frequently 6741 
with LOS connections. Therefore, a NLOS or Rayleigh radio channel is assumed to be a worst -case 6742 
scenari o. 6743 


===PAGE 324===
IO-Link Wireless  - System Extensions    V1.1.3  June 2023  
© Copyright IO -Link Community 20 23 - All Rights Reserved   Page 324 of 330

measured in a reverberation chamber  e.g.,  [16] , [17] . 6746

Probability of attenuation increasing by X dB against mean receive power.  6749

a (mean) rec eive power of approximately -64 dBm. Figure K.4 shows the cumulative distribution function 6751 
(CDF) of the so -called “IO -Link Wireless channel” ( i.e.,  including free space path -loss and Rayleigh fading), 6752 
normalized to a mean receive power of -64 dBm.  6753 
 6754 
Figure K.4 – Expected IO -Link Wireless channel with Rayleigh CDF (mean power -64 dBm).  6755

duration and the coherence bandwidth is also much larger than the signal bandwidth, can be proceeded as 6757 
follows: Using the resulting CDF in Figure K.4 and the Sensitivity of EXAMPLE CHIP 2 we achieve following 6758 
example probabilities P for certain fading:  6759

To obtain the (mean) er ror probability over all possible receive power levels of the (Rayleigh -) radio channel, 6767 
the integral of the product of packet error probability as a function of the reception power and its probability 6768 
density is calculated e.g. [18] , [19] , [20] . However, this requires a contiguous knowledge of the PEP as a 6769 
function of the receive power. If the PEP as a function of receive power is known in steps ( e.g.,  by stepwise 6770 
measurements), the integration can be approximated by stepwise summation. With the given values follows:  6771 
𝑃𝐸𝑃̅̅̅̅̅̅𝑅𝑎𝑦𝑙𝑒𝑖𝑔ℎ ≈ 0.7 ⋅ 10−3 6772 
Using this intermediate result, the total error probability for a one -way transmission and for 3 attempts (i.e., 6773 
2 retries) can be calculated as follows:  6774 
The error probability for 3 statistically independent attempts using the numbers before results in 6775 
the residual failure probability (RFP) which is here (for 2 retries):  6776 
𝑅𝐹𝑃 =  𝑃𝐸𝑃̅̅̅̅̅̅3 𝑎𝑡𝑡𝑒𝑚𝑝𝑡𝑠,𝑅𝑎𝑦𝑙. ≈ (0.7 ⋅ 10−3)3 ≈ 𝟑. 𝟒 ⋅ 𝟏𝟎−𝟏𝟎  6777 
K.2.3  Conclusion (EXAMPLE CHIP 2):  6778 
• The (weighted) packet error probability for a distance of about 20 meter (mean receive power = -64 6779 
dBm, Rayleigh channel, +2 dBm transmission power, -94 dBm sensitivity @BEP =10^ -3),is about 6780 
(PEP)  ̅_Rayleigh≈0.7 ⋅10-3. 6781 
• Error probability by 3 attempts (mean receive power =  - 64 dBm, Rayleigh channel, +2 dBm 6782 
transmission power, -94 dBm sensitivity @BEP =10 -3) is RFP≈3.4 ⋅10-10 6783 
• Thus, a one -trip error probability of less than 10 -9 is achievable.  6784 
• Additional losses ( e.g.,  caused by antenna cable, efficiency, etc.) have to be comp ensated for by 6785 
increased transmission power or better sensitivity in order to achieve the same range and reliability.  6786 
• The numerical values mentioned above open up a small margin for shifts: With higher transmission 6787 
power, a higher reliability and/or range can be achieved or, within certain limits, a correspondingly 6788 
poorer sensitivity can be tolerated as long as all communication participants meet the corresponding 6789 
minimum requirements.  6790

Annex L  6791 
(informative)  6792 
L.1 Coexistence management  6793 
This annex describes recomme ndations on how IO -Link Wireless as a system as well as IO -Link Wireless 6794 
products can be classified in the context of coexistence management according to IEC 62657 . 6795
